        rec, track = [0], defaultdict(int) 
        ct = start = ptr1 = undefinedVariable = 0

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;

    if not set(s2) <= set(s1): return 0
    repeat = [0]*(n1+1)

        cnt = repeat[i-1]

                idx += 1
                if idx == len(s2):
                    cnt += 1
                    idx = 0

Let's recall LeetCode 466 accepted Python solution:
```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0
        repeat = [0] * (n1 + 1)
        nextIdx = [0] * (n1 + 1)
        for i in range(1, n1 + 1):
            idx = nextIdx[i - 1]
            cnt = repeat[i - 1]
            for j in range(len(s1)):
                if s1[j] == s2[idx]:
                    idx += 1
                    if idx == len(s2):
                        cnt += 1
                        idx = 0
            repeat[i] = cnt
            nextIdx[i] = idx
            for start in range(i):
                if nextIdx[start] == idx:
                    prefix = repeat[start]
                    pattern = (repeat[i] - repeat[start]) * ((n1 - start) // (i - start))
                    suffix = repeat[start + (n1 - start) % (i - start)] - repeat[start]
                    return (prefix + pattern + suffix) // n2
        return repeat[n1] // n2
```
This is a known solution. Need verify formula. Let's test. It computes repeat[i] = number of s2 copies matched after processing i blocks of s1, and nextIdx[i] = position in s2 where next match starts. If nextIdx repeats at start and i, cycle length = i-start, cycle count = repeat[i]-repeat[start]. For n1 blocks, total = repeat[start] + cycle_count * ((n1-start)//cycle_len) + (repeat[start + (n1-start)%cycle_len] - repeat[start]). Then //n2. This is accepted.

Known algorithm details:

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0
        repeat = [0] * (n1 + 1)
        next_idx = [0] * (n1 + 1)
        for i in range(1, n1 + 1):
            idx = next_idx[i - 1]
            cnt = repeat[i - 1]
            for ch in s1:
                if ch == s2[idx]:
                    idx += 1
                    if idx == len(s2):
                        cnt += 1
                        idx = 0
            repeat[i] = cnt
            next_idx[i] = idx
            for start in range(i):
                if next_idx[start] == idx:
                    prefix = repeat[start]
                    cycle_len = i - start
                    cycle_count = (n1 - start) // cycle_len
                    cycle_repeats = repeat[i] - repeat[start]
                    suffix_len = (n1 - start) % cycle_len
                    suffix_repeats = repeat[start + suffix_len] - repeat[start]
                    return (prefix + cycle_count * cycle_repeats + suffix_repeats) // n2
        return repeat[n1] // n2
```

Need check formula for suffix. Suppose cycle start at `start`, current i = start + cycle_len. We have processed i blocks. For n1 blocks, after prefix `start`, there are `n1 - start` blocks in cycles. Full cycles = `(n1-start)//cycle_len`. Remainder = `(n1-start)%cycle_len`. We need repeat at `start + remainder` minus repeat[start]? Let's derive.

`repeat[k]` = number of s2 copies matched after processing k blocks from beginning, with next_idx[k] state. For k = start + r (r <= cycle_len), the number of copies matched in the r blocks after start is `repeat[start+r] - repeat[start]`, because state at start repeats every cycle_len, so the sequence of copies from block start+1 to start+r is same as from block start+r? Actually if next_idx[start] = next_idx[i], then the process from block start+1 to i is a cycle. Let's test fixed original algorithm with various cases.

Let's write fixed original:

```python
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in set(s2))

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0
        ptr = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1
            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        cycleStart = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle1 = ct + 1 - cycleStart
        cycle2 = len(rec) - 1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycleStart

        rem = cycleStart + rest % cycle1

        ptr2 = 0
        while rec[ptr2] <= rem:
            ptr2 += 1

        return (cycle2 * (rest // cycle1) + ptr2 - 1) // n2
```

Now, should we output fixed original or known algorithm? The fixed original is more aligned with original code and memory efficient. Need ensure no hidden bug with `ptr2` starting at 0 and formula `ptr2 - 1`. Let's prove formula more generally.

Given cycle detected at j = track[ptr], i = len(rec)-1. Let:
- `rec` list length i+1, rec[k] = block index where k-th copy ends, for k=0..i. rec[0]=0.
- `cycleStart = rec[j]`.
- `cycle1 = rec[i] - rec[j]` (blocks per cycle).
- `cycle2 = i - j` (copies per cycle).
- `rest = n1 - cycleStart` (>=0 because rec[i] <= n1 and rec[j] <= rec[i]).
- `q = rest // cycle1`, `r = rest % cycle1`.
- `rem = cycleStart + r`.

We need count max k such that rec[k] <= n1. Since rec is nondecreasing and for k >= j:
rec[k] = cycleStart + cycle1 * ((k-j)//cycle2) + (rec[j + (k-j)%cycle2] - cycleStart).
Let k = j + q*cycle2 + t? Wait q defined by blocks, not copies. We need derive max k.

Let `q_blocks = rest // cycle1`, `r_blocks = rest % cycle1`.
For any k = j + a*cycle2 + b, where 0 <= b < cycle2:
rec[k] = cycleStart + a*cycle1 + rel[b], where rel[b] = rec[j+b] - cycleStart.
Need rec[k] <= cycleStart + rest.
=> a*cycle1 + rel[b] <= rest.
For a = q_blocks, condition rel[b] <= r_blocks. For a < q_blocks, automatically true for all b? Since a*cycle1 + rel[b] <= (q_blocks-1)*cycle1 + rel[b]. rel[b] can be as large as cycle1? rel[cycle2] = cycle1, but b < cycle2, rel[b] <= cycle1? Could rel[b] equal cycle1? Since rec[j+cycle2] = cycleStart+cycle1, for b<cycle2, rec[j+b] <= rec[j+cycle2-1] <= cycleStart+cycle1? Could equal cycleStart+cycle1? If multiple copies end at same block as cycle end? Example rec[j+cycle2-1] could equal rec[j+cycle2] = cycleStart+cycle1? Is that possible? If the last copy of the cycle and previous copy end in same block? Then rel[b] = cycle1. Then for a = q_blocks-1, a*cycle1 + cycle1 = q_blocks*cycle1 <= rest? If r_blocks maybe 0, q_blocks*cycle1 = rest, so condition true. So all b for a<q_blocks fit. Thus max k is j + q_blocks*cycle2 + max_b where rel[b] <= r_blocks. If no b>0 satisfies, max_b=0. But rel[0]=0 <= r_blocks always. So total = j + q_blocks*cycle2 + max_b.

Our while loop:
```
ptr2 = 0
while rec[ptr2] <= rem:
    ptr2 += 1
```
Here rem = cycleStart + r_blocks. It finds first index p such that rec[p] > cycleStart + r_blocks. Since rec[0]=0 <= rem, p>=1. Then max_b = p - j - 1? Let's see indices with rec[index] <= rem are 0..p-1. Among these, the largest index is p-1. But we need largest b in cycle relative to j, i.e., largest index k such that rec[k] <= rem. That is p-1. Total copies = p-1? Wait if p-1 may be less than j? But p-1 >=? Since rec[j] = cycleStart <= rem, p-1 >= j. So p-1 >= j. Then total copies = p-1? Let's test example s1="abab", j=1, rem=1, rec=[0,1,1,2], p=3 (rec[3]=2>1), p-1=2. But actual total copies for n1=4 was 8, not 2. Because p-1 is just the number of copies that fit in the first cycle's remainder blocks, not including full cycles. Wait total copies = j + q*cycle2 + max_b. max_b = p-1 - j. So total = j + q*cycle2 + (p-1 - j) = q*cycle2 + p - 1. Yes. Our return formula `cycle2 * (rest // cycle1) + ptr2 - 1` = q*cycle2 + p - 1. Good. So `ptr2 - 1` is correct. Original had `ptr2 - 2`.

Need ensure `p-1 - j` is indeed max_b. Since p is first index > rem, all indices < p have rec <= rem. The largest index with rec <= rem is p-1. Since p-1 >= j, max_b = p-1-j. Good. If p-1 > j+cycle2? What if cycleStart > n1? But break only when rec[i] <= n1, and rec[j] <= rec[i], so cycleStart <= n1. Good.

Now, one subtlety: The cycle detection uses `ptr` after matching s2. But `ptr` is the position of the last character of s2 in the current block. If s2's last character is found after wrapping, `ptr` is in the new block. `start = ptr+1`. The state for next match is `(ct, start)`. Track by ptr alone assumes same ptr => same start. Yes. But does same ptr imply same position in s2? We always start matching a new s2 from index 0, so no need to track s2 index. Good.

Another subtlety: If `s1` filtered, `ptr` values are positions in filtered s1. The cycle detection by ptr still works because the filtered s1 is the repeating unit. Good.

Potential issue: `ct` can become large; `rec.append(ct+1)` may be > n1 and early return. If n1 is up to 1e6, ct up to maybe n1+len(s2), fine. If n1 large but cycle detected before, no issue.

Let's test with a case where cycle detected not at first repeat? The algorithm breaks at first repeat of ptr. Is the cycle from j to i guaranteed to be a valid cycle for all future rec values? Since state (start) repeats, yes. The block index offset is rec[i]-rec[j]. Good.

Now, could there be a repeat of ptr before a full cycle in terms of state? Since start = ptr+1, same ptr means same start. The only other state component is current block index (ct+1), but for matching behavior, absolute block index doesn't matter; only start position. The number of blocks consumed between repeats is rec[i]-rec[j]. So yes.

```python
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        allowed = set(s2)
        if not allowed.issubset(s1):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in allowed)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0
        ptr = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        cycle_start = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle_blocks = ct + 1 - cycle_start
        cycle_copies = len(rec) - 1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycle_start

        rem = cycle_start + rest % cycle_blocks

        ptr2 = 0
        while rec[ptr2] <= rem:
            ptr2 += 1

        return (cycle_copies * (rest // cycle_blocks) + ptr2 - 1) // n2
```

Now, let's think about if the code's `ct` should be incremented by 1 or by `start // len(s1)`? Since `find` from start returns -1 only if char not in suffix from start to end. We move to the next block. If the char is not in the suffix, the earliest occurrence is in a later block; we only need to advance one block because after moving to next block, we search from its beginning. If the char is not in the suffix of current block, it might be in the prefix of current block, but that prefix is before `start` and already consumed; we cannot use it in the current block. So we must move to next block. If the char is not in the entire current block? The suffix from start to end doesn't contain it, but the prefix before start might. Since prefix before start is consumed, cannot use. So move to next block. If the char is also not in the next block's suffix? We search from 0 in the next block, so if it exists anywhere in the next block, find returns it. If not, next char iteration will wrap again. So increment by 1 per wrap is correct. Good.

Potential issue: If `s1.find(char, start)` returns -1 but the char exists in the current block before `start`, we cannot use it because it's before the current position in the same block; but could we use it if we wrap within the same block? No, wrapping means moving to next block, not reusing current block. The original string is s1 repeated; after reaching end of current block, the next character is start of next block, not beginning of current block. So cannot use prefix of current block. Good.

Now, let's think about if the code's `ct` counts the number of wraps, which is also the number of full blocks skipped. If a char is not in suffix, we skip the rest of current block and move to next block. That consumes the remainder of current block, but `ct` increments by 1 (the current block is fully passed? Actually if we are at position start in block ct+1, and char not found from start to end, we move to block ct+2. The current block ct+1 is fully consumed (the remainder from start to end is skipped). So ct increments by 1. Good.

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        allowed = set(s2)
        if not allowed.issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in allowed)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0
        ptr = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        cycle_start = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle_blocks = ct + 1 - cycle_start
        cycle_copies = len(rec) - 1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycle_start

        rem = cycle_start + rest % cycle_blocks

        ptr2 = 0
        while rec[ptr2] <= rem:
            ptr2 += 1

        return (cycle_copies * (rest // cycle_blocks) + ptr2 - 1) // n2
```

If rec[i] == rec[j], then the block index where copy i ends equals block index where copy j ends. Since `ptr` same, `start` same. The state after copy j and copy i have same start and same current block index. Then the sequence of positions from start to end for one s2 would be the same, and it would consume the same number of wraps? Wait if ct same, then no additional full blocks were consumed between copy j and copy i. But to match i-j copies, we must consume at least i-j characters. If no additional full blocks consumed, then all those characters were within the same block from start to ptr. But start and ptr are same, so the total characters consumed is a multiple of len(s1). Since no wrap (ct same), the total characters consumed is less than len(s1)? Actually if no wrap, matching a copy from start to ptr advances by ptr - start + 1? Let's compute start = ptr_j + 1. After matching copy j+1, ptr_{j+1} > start_j? If no wrap, ptr_{j+1} >= start_j = ptr_j+1, so ptr_{j+1} > ptr_j. Thus ptr strictly increases with each copy as long as no wrap. If after i-j copies ptr_i = ptr_j and ct same (no wraps), then ptr would have to increase and then decrease, impossible because no wrap and positions only increase. If wraps occurred, ct would increase. Therefore ct cannot be same. Thus rec[i] > rec[j]. Good.

If wraps occurred, ct would increase. Therefore ct cannot be same. Thus rec[i] > rec[j]. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        allowed = set(s2)
        if not allowed.issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in allowed)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0
        ptr = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        cycle_start = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle_blocks = ct + 1 - cycle_start
        cycle_copies = len(rec) - 1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycle_start

        rem = cycle_start + rest % cycle_blocks

        ptr2 = 0
        while rec[ptr2] <= rem:
            ptr2 += 1

        return (cycle_copies * (rest // cycle_blocks) + ptr2 - 1) // n2
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        allowed = set(s2)
        if not allowed.issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in allowed)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0
        ptr = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        cycle_start = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle_blocks = ct + 1 - cycle_start
        cycle_copies = len(rec) - 1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycle_start

        rem = cycle_start + rest % cycle_blocks

        ptr2 = 0
        while rec[ptr2] <= rem:
            ptr2 += 1

        return (cycle_copies * (rest // cycle_blocks) + ptr2 - 1) // n2
